668	FUNCTION {format.full.names}
669	{'s :=
670	 "" 't :=
671	  #1 'nameptr :=
672	  s num.names$ 'numnames :=
673	  numnames 'namesleft :=
674	    { namesleft #0 > }
675	    { s nameptr
676	      "{vv~}{ll}" format.name$
677	      't :=
678	      nameptr #1 >
679	        {
680	          namesleft #1 >
681	            { ", " * t * }
682	            {
683	              s nameptr "{ll}" format.name$ duplicate$ "others" =
684	                { 't := }
685	                { pop$ }
686	              if$
687	              t "others" =
688	                {
689	                  " " * bbl.etal *
690	                }
691	                {
692	                  numnames #2 >
693	                    { "," * }
694	                    'skip$
695	                  if$
696	                  bbl.and
697	                  space.word * t *
698	                }
699	              if$
700	            }
701	          if$
702	        }
703	        't
704	      if$
705	      nameptr #1 + 'nameptr :=
706	      namesleft #1 - 'namesleft :=
707	    }
708	  while$
709	}

777	FUNCTION {n.dashify}
778	{
779	  't :=
780	  ""
781	    { t empty$ not }
782	    { t #1 #1 substring$ "-" =
783	        { t #1 #2 substring$ "--" = not
784	            { "--" *
785	              t #2 global.max$ substring$ 't :=
786	            }
787	            {   { t #1 #1 substring$ "-" = }
788	                { "-" *
789	                  t #2 global.max$ substring$ 't :=
790	                }
791	              while$
792	            }
793	          if$
794	        }
795	        { t #1 #1 substring$ *
796	          t #2 global.max$ substring$ 't :=
797	        }
798	      if$
799	    }
800	  while$
801	}

842	    }

853	                { bbl.number capitalize }
854	              if$

856	              bbl.in space.word *
857	              series "series" bibinfo.check *
858	            }
859	          if$

879	INTEGERS { multiresult }
880	FUNCTION {multi.page.check}
881	{ 't :=
882	  #0 'multiresult :=
883	    { multiresult not
884	      t empty$ not
885	      and
886	    }
887	    { t #1 #1 substring$
888	      duplicate$ "-" =
889	      swap$ duplicate$ "," =
890	      swap$ "+" =
891	      or or
892	        { #1 'multiresult := }
893	        { t #2 global.max$ substring$ 't := }
894	      if$
895	    }
896	  while$
897	  multiresult
898	}
899	FUNCTION {format.pages}
900	{ pages duplicate$ empty$ 'skip$
901	    { duplicate$ multi.page.check
902	        {
903	          bbl.pages swap$
904	          n.dashify
905	        }
906	        {
907	          bbl.page swap$
908	        }
909	      if$
910	      tie.or.space.prefix
911	      "pages" bibinfo.check
912	      * *
913	    }
914	  if$
915	}

925	          *
926	        }

933	  duplicate$ empty$ 'pop$

943	FUNCTION {format.vol.num.pages}
944	{ volume field.or.null
945	  duplicate$ empty$ 'skip$
946	    {
947	      "volume" bibinfo.check
948	    }
949	  if$
950	  number "number" bibinfo.check duplicate$ empty$ 'skip$
951	    {
952	      swap$ duplicate$ empty$
953	        { "there's a number but no volume in " cite$ * warning$ }
954	        'skip$
955	      if$
956	      swap$
957	      "(" swap$ * ")" *
958	    }
959	  if$ *
960	  eid empty$
961	    { format.journal.pages }
962	    { format.journal.eid }
963	  if$
964	}

973	        }

997	      if$

1020	          " " *
1021	          get.bbl.editor

1023	          * swap$

1046	    { pop$ "t" change.case$ }

1050	FUNCTION {format.article.crossref}
1051	{
1052	  word.in

1054	}
1055	FUNCTION {format.book.crossref}
1056	{ volume duplicate$ empty$
1057	    { "empty volume in " cite$ * "'s crossref of " * crossref * warning$
1058	      pop$ word.in
1059	    }
1060	    { bbl.volume
1061	      capitalize
1062	      swap$ tie.or.space.prefix "volume" bibinfo.check * * bbl.of space.word *
1063	    }
1064	  if$
1065	  " \cite{" * crossref * "}" *
1066	}

1069	  word.in
1070	  " \cite{" * crossref * "}" *
1071	}
1072	FUNCTION {format.org.or.pub}
1073	{ 't :=
1074	  ""
1075	  address empty$ t empty$ and

1078	      t empty$
1079	        { address "address" bibinfo.check *
1080	        }
1081	        { t *
1082	          address empty$
1083	            'skip$
1084	            { ", " * address "address" bibinfo.check * }
1085	          if$
1086	        }
1087	      if$
1088	    }
1089	  if$

1099	FUNCTION {archiveprefix.or.eprinttype} % holder for eprinttype with archiveprefix precedence
1100	{
1101	  archiveprefix empty$
1102	  {
1103	    eprinttype empty$
1104	      { "" } % not using 'skip$ to reduce errors like "nothing to pop from stack"
1105	      { eprinttype }
1106	    if$
1107	  }
1108	  { archiveprefix }
1109	  if$
1110	}
1111	
1112	FUNCTION {output.eprint} % this is only used with the @misc record type (common for arXiv and other preprint server bibtex records)
1113	{
1114	  eprint empty$
1115	    {% if eprint field is empty
1116	      publisher field.or.null "arXiv" = % field.or.null here helps when no publisher field in the record
1117	        { publisher " preprint" * } % add " preprint" to publisher with the idea that publisher is the name of the preprint server
1118	        { "" } % if publisher != "arXiv" then empty output
1119	      if$
1120	      emphasize % no output function after emphasize because nothing goes after this
1121	    }
1122	    {% if eprint field is not empty
1123	      archiveprefix.or.eprinttype empty$
1124	        { "" } % not using 'skip$ to reduce errors like "nothing to pop from stack"
1125	        {% if archiveprefix or eprinttype fields are not empty
1126	          journal empty$
1127	            { "Preprint" } % if journal field is empty: output just "Preprint" emphasized like a journal name
1128	            { journal } % if journal field is not empty, output it (takes precedence)
1129	          if$
1130	          emphasize output % emphasize what we formed before, setting output as a border to the subblock that follows with the comma delimiter
1131	          archiveprefix.or.eprinttype ":" * eprint * % subblock with eprinttype and eprint number
1132	        }
1133	      if$
1134	    }
1135	  if$
1136	}

1138	% urlbst...
1139	% Functions for making hypertext links.
1140	% In all cases, the stack has (link-text href-url)
1141	%
1142	% make 'null' specials
1143	FUNCTION {make.href.null}
1144	{
1145	  pop$
1146	}
1147	% make hypertex specials
1148	FUNCTION {make.href.hypertex}
1149	{
1150	  "\special {html:&lt;a href=" quote$ *
1151	  swap$ * quote$ * "> }" * swap$ *
1152	  "\special {html:&lt;/a>}" *
1153	}
1154	% make hyperref specials
1155	FUNCTION {make.href.hyperref}
1156	{
1157	  "\href {" swap$ * "} {\path{" * swap$ * "}}" *
1158	}
1159	FUNCTION {make.href}
1160	{ hrefform #2 =
1161	    'make.href.hyperref      % hrefform = 2
1162	    { hrefform #1 =
1163	        'make.href.hypertex  % hrefform = 1
1164	        'make.href.null      % hrefform = 0 (or anything else)
1165	      if$
1166	    }
1167	  if$
1168	}
1169	
1170	% If inlinelinks is true, then format.url should be a no-op, since it's
1171	% (a) redundant, and (b) could end up as a link-within-a-link.
1172	FUNCTION {format.url}
1173	{ inlinelinks #1 = url empty$ or
1174	   { "" }
1175	   { hrefform #1 =
1176	       { % special case -- add HyperTeX specials
1177	         urlintro "\url{" url * "}" * url make.href.hypertex * }
1178	       { urlintro "\url{" * url * "}" * }
1179	     if$
1180	   }
1181	  if$
1182	}
1183	FUNCTION {format.eprint}
1184	{ eprint empty$
1185	    { "" }
1186	    { eprintprefix eprint * eprinturl eprint * make.href }
1187	  if$
1188	}
1189	
1190	FUNCTION {format.doi}
1191	{ doi empty$
1192	    { "" }
1193	    { doi parse.doi % leaves "https://doi.org/DOI" DOI on the stack
1194	      's := 't :=
1195	      doiform #1 =
1196	        { "\doi{" s * "}" * }
1197	        { doiprefix s * t make.href }
1198	      if$
1199	    }
1200	  if$
1201	}
1202	
1203	FUNCTION {format.pubmed}
1204	{ pubmed empty$
1205	    { "" }
1206	    { pubmedprefix pubmed * pubmedurl pubmed * make.href }
1207	  if$
1208	}
1209	
1210	% Output a URL.  We can't use the more normal idiom (something like
1211	% `format.url output'), because the `inbrackets' within
1212	% format.lastchecked applies to everything between calls to `output',
1213	% so that `format.url format.lastchecked * output' ends up with both
1214	% the URL and the lastchecked in brackets.
1215	FUNCTION {output.url}
1216	{ url empty$
1217	    'skip$
1218	    { new.block
1219	      format.url output
1220	      format.lastchecked output
1221	    }
1222	  if$
1223	}
1224	
1225	FUNCTION {output.web.refs}
1226	{
1227	  new.block
1228	  inlinelinks
1229	    'skip$ % links were inline -- don't repeat them
1230	    { % If the generated DOI will be the same as the URL,
1231	      % then don't print the URL (thanks to Joseph Wright
1232	      % for (the original version of) this code,
1233	      % at http://tex.stackexchange.com/questions/5660)
1234	      adddoi
1235	          doi empty$ { "X" } { doi parse.doi pop$ } if$ % DOI URL to be generated
1236	          url empty$ { "Y" } { url } if$          % the URL, or "Y" if empty
1237	          =                                       % are the strings equal?
1238	          and
1239	        'skip$
1240	        { output.url }
1241	      if$
1242	      addeprints eprint empty$ not and
1243	        { format.eprint output.nonnull }
1244	        'skip$
1245	      if$
1246	      adddoi doi empty$ not and
1247	        { format.doi output.nonnull }
1248	        'skip$
1249	      if$
1250	      addpubmed pubmed empty$ not and
1251	        { format.pubmed output.nonnull }
1252	        'skip$
1253	      if$
1254	    }
1255	  if$
1256	}
1257	
1258	% Wrapper for output.bibitem.original.
1259	% If the URL field is not empty, set makeinlinelink to be true,
1260	% so that an inline link will be started at the next opportunity
1261	FUNCTION {output.bibitem}
1262	{ outside.brackets 'bracket.state :=
1263	  output.bibitem.original
1264	  inlinelinks url empty$ not doi empty$ not or pubmed empty$ not or eprint empty$ not or and
1265	    { #1 'makeinlinelink := }
1266	    { #0 'makeinlinelink := }
1267	  if$
1268	}
1269	
1270	% Wrapper for fin.entry.original
1271	FUNCTION {fin.entry}
1272	{ output.web.refs  % urlbst
1273	  makeinlinelink       % ooops, it appears we didn't have a title for inlinelink
1274	    { possibly.setup.inlinelink % add some artificial link text here, as a fallback
1275	      linktextstring output.nonnull }
1276	    'skip$
1277	  if$
1278	  bracket.state close.brackets = % urlbst
1279	    { "]" * }
1280	    'skip$
1281	  if$
1282	  fin.entry.original
1283	}

1285	% Webpage entry type.
1286	% Title and url fields required;
1287	% author, note, year, month, and lastchecked fields optional
1288	% See references
1289	%   ISO 690-2 http://www.nlc-bnc.ca/iso/tc46sc9/standard/690-2e.htm
1290	%   http://www.classroom.net/classroom/CitingNetResources.html
1291	%   http://neal.ctstateu.edu/history/cite.html
1292	%   http://www.cas.usf.edu/english/walker/mla.html
1293	% for citation formats for web pages.
1294	FUNCTION {webpage}
1295	{ output.bibitem
1296	  author empty$
1297	    { editor empty$
1298	        'skip$  % author and editor both optional
1299	        { format.editors output.nonnull }
1300	      if$
1301	    }
1302	    { editor empty$
1303	        { format.authors output.nonnull }
1304	        { "can't use both author and editor fields in " cite$ * warning$ }
1305	      if$
1306	    }
1307	  if$
1308	  new.block
1309	  title empty$ 'skip$ 'possibly.setup.inlinelink if$
1310	  format.title "title" output.check
1311	  inbrackets onlinestring output
1312	  new.block
1313	  year empty$
1314	    'skip$
1315	    { format.date "year" output.check }
1316	  if$
1317	  % We don't need to output the URL details ('lastchecked' and 'url'),
1318	  % because fin.entry does that for us, using output.web.refs.  The only
1319	  % reason we would want to put them here is if we were to decide that
1320	  % they should go in front of the rather miscellaneous information in 'note'.
1321	  new.block
1322	  note output
1323	  fin.entry
1324	}

cite$ * warning$\n        }\n        { swap$\n          pop$\n        }\n      if$\n    }\n  if$\n}\nINTEGERS { nameptr namesleft numnames }\n\n\nSTRINGS  { bibinfo}\n\nFUNCTION {format.names}\n{ 'bibinfo :=\n  duplicate$ empty$ 'skip$ {\n  's :=\n  \"\" 't :=\n  #1 'nameptr :=\n  s num.names$ 'numnames :=\n  numnames 'namesleft :=\n    { namesleft #0 > }\n    { s nameptr\n      \"{ff~}{vv~}{ll}{, jj}\" % first name first for all authors\n      format.name$\n      bibinfo bibinfo.check\n      't :=\n      nameptr #1 >\n        {\n          nameptr #19\t% truncate after 19 names\n          #1 + =\n          numnames #20\t% if there are more than 20 names\n          > and\n            { \"others\" 't :=\n              #1 'namesleft := }\n            'skip$\n          if$\t\t% end truncation of long list of names\n          namesleft #1 >\n            { \", \" * t * }\n            {\n              s nameptr \"{ll}\" format.name$ duplicate$ \"others\" =\n 'nameptr :=\n      namesleft #1 - 'namesleft :=\n    }\n  while$\n  } if$\n}\nFUNCTION {format.names.ed}\n{\n  format.names\n}\nFUNCTION {format.key}\n{ empty$\n    { key field.or.null }\n    { \"\" }\n  if$\n}\n\nFUNCTION {format.authors}\n{ author \"author\" format.names\n}\nFUNCTION {get.bbl.editor}\n{ editor num.names$ #1 > 'bbl.editors 'bbl.editor if$ }\n\nFUNCTION {format.editors}\n{ editor bibinfo.check\n}\nFUNCTION {format.full.names}\n{'s :=\n \"\" 't :=\n  #1 'nameptr :=\n  s num.names$ 'numnames :=\n  numnames 'namesleft :=\n    { namesleft #0 > }\n    { s nameptr\n format.full.names }\n      if$\n    }\n    { author format.full.names }\n  if$\n}\n\nFUNCTION {author.key.full}\n{ author empty$\n    { key empty$\n         { cite$ #1 #3 substring$ }\n before.all 'output.state :=\n}\n\nFUNCTION {n.dashify}\n{\n  't :=\n  \"\"\n    { t empty$ not }\n    { t #1 #1 substring$ \"-\" =\n        { t #1 #2 substring$ \"--\" = not\n            { \"--\" *\n      }\n              while$\n            }\n          if$\n        }\n        { t #1 #1 substring$ *\n          t #2 global.max$ substring$ 't :=\n        }\n      if$\n    }\n  while$\n}\n\nFUNCTION {word.in}\n{ bbl.in capitalize\n  \" \" * }\n\nFUNCTION {format.date}\n{ year \"year\" bibinfo.check duplicate$ empty$\n    {\n    }\n    'skip$\n  if$\n  extra.label *\n  before.all 'output.state empty$\n    'pop$\n    { \"can't use both \" swap$ * \" fields in \" * cite$ * warning$ }\n  if$\n}\nFUNCTION {format.bvolume}\n{ volume empty$\n    { \"\" }\n    { bbl.volume volume empty$\n            { number \"number\" bibinfo.check }\n            { output.state mid.sentence =\n                { bbl.number }\n                { bbl.number capitalize }\n              if$\n empty$\n    'format.pages\n    { type empty$\n        { bbl.chapter }\n        { type \"l\" change.case$\n          \"type\" bibinfo.check\n        }\n      if$\n      chapter tie.or.space.prefix\n preprint\" * } % add \" preprint\" to publisher with the idea that publisher is the name of the preprint server\n        { \"\" } % if publisher != \"arXiv\" then empty output\n      if$\n 'skip$ to reduce errors like \"nothing to pop from stack\"\n        {% if archiveprefix or eprinttype fields are not empty\n          journal empty$\n            { \"Preprint\" } % if journal field is empty: output just \"Preprint\" emphasized like a journal name\n            { journal } % if journal field is not empty, output it (takes precedence)\n          if$\n          emphasize output % emphasize what we formed before, setting output as a border to the subblock that follows with the comma delimiter\n          archiveprefix.or.eprinttype \":\" * eprint * % subblock with eprinttype and eprint number\n        }\n      if$\n    }\n  if$\n}\n\n% urlbst...\n% Functions for making hypertext links.\n% In all cases, the stack has (link-text href-url)\n%\n% make 'null' specials\nFUNCTION {make.href.null}\n{\n  pop$\n}\n% make hypertex specials\nFUNCTION {make.href.hypertex}\n{\n  \"\\special {html:&lt;a href=\" quote$ *\n  swap$ * quote$ * \"> }\" * swap$ *\n  \"\\special {html:&lt;/a>}\" *\n}\n% make hyperref specials\nFUNCTION {make.href.hyperref}\n{\n  \"\\href {\" swap$ * \"} {\\path{\" * swap$ * \"}}\" *\n}\nFUNCTION {make.href}\n{ hrefform #2 =\n * }\n     if$\n   }\n  if$\n}\nFUNCTION {format.eprint}\n{ eprint empty$\n    { \"\" }\n    { eprintprefix eprint * eprinturl eprint * make.href }\n  if$\n}\n\nFUNCTION {format.doi}\n{ doi empty$\n and\n    { #1 'makeinlinelink := }\n    { #0 'makeinlinelink := }\n  if$\n}\n\n% Wrapper for fin.entry.original\nFUNCTION {fin.entry}\n{ output.web.refs  % urlbst\n  makeinlinelink       % ooops, it bracket.state close.brackets = % urlbst\n    { \"]\" * }\n    'skip$\n  if$\n  fin.entry.original\n}\n\n% Webpage entry type.\n% Title and url fields required;\n% author, note, year, month, and \"can't use both author and editor fields in \" cite$ * warning$ }\n      if$\n    }\n  if$\n  new.block\n  title empty$ 'skip$ 'possibly.setup.inlinelink if$\n  format.title \"title\" output.check\n \"year\" output.check\n  date.block\n  title empty$ 'skip$ 'possibly.setup.inlinelink if$ % urlbst\n  format.btitle \"title\" output.check\n  crossref missing$\n    {\n      format.edition output\n {

  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Introduction}\n\nExecution traces (line-by-line records of a program's runtime behavior) have emerged as a promising input modality for code language models \\citep{chen2025tracepile,ding2024traced,wang2025dcsh,ni2024next}.\nYet feeding thousands of trace lines to a model can be no better than feeding none: the diagnostic signal drowns in routine execution steps.\n\\emph{Which trace components actually carry the signal that matters?}\n\nPrior work found no single trace representation consistently outperforms others \\citep{wang2025dcsh}, but focused on generation-oriented tasks.\n\\emph{Verdict prediction}---classifying whether a test passes or fails---offers a cleaner testbed: as binary classification, it isolates the learning signal from generation noise, enabling controlled component ablation.\nAs fault detection, the model need not simulate execution, only determine whether something went wrong---and if that signal is concentrated in a small trace subset, information density should matter more than quantity.\n\nWe test this hypothesis through the first systematic component ablation of trace representations for verdict prediction.\nUsing 591 problems from HumanEval \\citep{chen2021evaluating} and MBPP-sanitized \\citep{austin2021program}, we decompose a multi-component trace representation (VOTA: exception events + assertion-point comparisons + loop compression) into individual components across four models spanning 1.5B to 13B parameters and three architecture families: Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B/1.5B-Instruct \\citep{qwen2025coder}, CodeLlama-13B-Instruct \\citep{roziere2024code}, and DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct \\citep{deepseek2024coder}.\nThe results are striking: complete traces (${\\sim}$80\\%) underperform a single compact component (${\\geq}$99\\%) despite containing strictly more information---a direct demonstration that information density, not quantity, determines learning efficiency.\n\n\\textbf{Exception events alone achieve ${\\geq}$99.33\\% verdict accuracy across all four models and all seeds tested} (\\S\\ref{sec:cross_model}).\nThis near-perfect accuracy holds from 1.5B to 13B parameters, while the code-only baseline (\\texttt{no\\_trace}) varies from 67.3\\% to 76.5\\% with strong scale dependence.\nTraining dynamics confirm the density hypothesis: high-density inputs converge rapidly and stably, while verbose traces oscillate without reaching stable accuracy even when gradient dilution is eliminated (Figure~\\ref{fig:training_dynamics}).\n\nAdding trace information beyond exceptions provides no benefit: VOTA (${\\geq}$98.57\\% across all models, excluding outlier seeds) slightly underperforms \\texttt{exception\\_only} on Qwen-7B (99.05\\% vs.\\ 99.83\\%), with both conditions achieving near-ceiling accuracy across architectures.\nThe performance gap approximately decomposes into information overload (${\\sim}$76\\%) and gradient dilution (${\\sim}$24\\%) on Qwen-7B (3 seeds).\nA tail-truncation budget sweep across four models reveals a phase transition at ${\\sim}$500 tokens, with \\texttt{tail\\_2048} recovering 95--98\\% of \\texttt{exception\\_only}'s accuracy.\nEven on silent logic errors (55.5\\% of failures), \\texttt{exception\\_only} maintains 100\\% recall because test assertions generate exception events.\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}\n\\item \\textbf{Information density determines SFT learning efficiency}: training dynamics directly show that denser trace components learn faster and more stably---\\texttt{exception\\_only} converges within 50 steps while \\texttt{full\\_trace} with label-only loss oscillates (82--89\\%) without stable convergence. Seed variance negatively correlates with input density ($\\sigma{=}$0.00 to 1.12), and SFT closes a 6--49pp zero-shot gap across all models (\\S\\ref{sec:ablation}, Appendix~\\ref{sec:training_dynamics}).\n\\item \\textbf{Exception events are the minimal sufficient signal}: ${\\geq}$99.33\\% across all four models and all seeds tested (1.5B--13B, three families), with ${\\sim}$91\\% semantic rather than format-driven (\\S\\ref{sec:cross_model}, \\S\\ref{sec:nomarker}). Cross-dataset evaluation on DebugBench ($N{=}426$) preserves the ranking, though margins narrow from ceiling effects (\\S\\ref{sec:debugbench}).\n\\item \\textbf{Approximate quantitative decomposition of the full-trace performance gap}: On Qwen-7B (3 seeds), the gap decomposes into gradient dilution (${\\sim}$24\\%) and information overload (${\\sim}$76\\%), implying content selection matters more than loss engineering. A budget sweep across four models reveals a phase transition at ${\\sim}$500 tail tokens (\\S\\ref{sec:density}, \\S\\ref{sec:trace_length}).\n\\item \\textbf{Two-tier diagnostic hierarchy}: Tier~1 signals (exceptions, assertions) generalize across all four models (${\\geq}$99.33\\%), while Tier~2 (loop compression) is model-dependent: Qwen +10.9/+11.0pp (7B/1.5B), DeepSeek +7.9pp, CodeLlama +0.6pp over no trace (\\S\\ref{sec:ablation}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",
        "numLines": 31,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 31
      }

{

  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Discussion}\n\nThe central finding is that information density determines learning efficiency for trace-based verdict prediction.\nException events concentrate the runtime's primary fault signal---error type, stack frame, and message---in a compact format (median 150 tokens), while non-exception components (variable states, loop iterations, control flow) record \\emph{how} the program ran, not \\emph{whether} it failed.\n\nThis density perspective explains three key results: (1)~adding trace information beyond exceptions provides no benefit (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}), because low-density content competes for attention; (2)~a tail-truncation budget sweep reveals a sharp phase transition at ${\\sim}$500 tokens (Table~\\ref{tab:tail_cross_model}), matching the boundary where diagnostic events dominate; (3)~the SFT advantage over regex concentrates on caught-exception boundary cases, confirming semantic signal extraction (\\S\\ref{sec:ablation}).\nThe narrow cross-model gap (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_model}) supports that signal density, not model capacity, is the binding constraint.\n\n\\paragraph{Why exception\\_only outperforms ae\\_only.}\nAlthough \\texttt{ae\\_only} is a strict superset, it underperforms \\texttt{exception\\_only} (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}), indicating extra content introduces noise rather than signal.\n\n\\paragraph{SFT vs.\\ regex: semantic understanding.}\nA regex baseline exploits a format marker for near-ceiling accuracy, but a \\emph{nomarker} ablation (\\S\\ref{sec:nomarker}) resolves this concern: removing markers drops accuracy by only ${\\sim}$2pp, leaving a ${>}$4pp gap over the best non-trivial regex.\nOn 8 boundary samples where pass-labeled code triggers exceptions, SFT classifies 6/8 correctly vs.\\ 0/8 for regex (McNemar $p{=}.041$), confirming ${\\sim}$91\\% of the signal is semantic.\n\n\\paragraph{Why zero-shot and few-shot prompting fall short.}\nZero-shot prompting spans a 42pp range across models (\\S\\ref{sec:additional}), which SFT compresses to sub-1pp; few-shot ICL improves marginally, confirming the bottleneck is verdict calibration rather than exception recognition.\n\n\\paragraph{Near-perfect floor, not hard ceiling.}\nThe 2 errors (both \\texttt{GeneratorExit}; Appendix~\\ref{sec:error_overlap}) are shared across configurations; CodeLlama-13B seed 123 achieves 100\\%, confirming 99.83\\% is a convergence \\emph{floor}, signal-limited rather than capacity-limited.\n\n\\paragraph{Tokenizer efficiency.}\nCodeLlama's tokenizer produces ${\\sim}$20\\% more tokens than Qwen's on identical inputs; a label-only loss variant recovers accuracy at tail-1024 but collapses at tail-2048, indicating LoRA long-sequence instability (Appendix~\\ref{sec:tokenizer_analysis}).\n\n\\paragraph{Tier hierarchy and methodological implications.}\nThe two-tier structure (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_model}) suggests a fallback design: extract exception events first, resort to Tier~2 only when no exception is raised.\nThe near-zero loop benefit on CodeLlama-13B may reflect limited loop-pattern coverage in its pre-training data, combined with a relatively high \\texttt{no\\_trace} baseline (76.47\\%) that leaves minimal headroom for loop-derived signal.\nNotably, the Qwen family shows consistent loop benefit across scale (1.5B: +11.0pp; 7B: +10.9pp), suggesting that loop signal utilization is an architecture-level property rather than a capacity effect.\nThe methodological insight---that information density determines learning efficiency---transfers to any trace-based task where compact diagnostic events exist within verbose traces, such as re-analyzing archived CI/CD logs when the original test environment is unavailable.\n\n\\paragraph{Density and cross-model robustness.}\nTier~1 and Tier~2 exhibit contrasting cross-model stability (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_model}): \\texttt{exception\\_only}'s cross-model range is ${<}$1pp, while \\texttt{loop\\_only} spans ${\\sim}$9pp.\nHowever, $N{=}3$ models is insufficient to establish a continuous relationship, and the correlation is confounded: Tier~1 signals are both high-density \\emph{and} direct fault indicators, so density cannot be disentangled from signal type.\nThis is further corroborated by a negative result: applying label-only loss to DeepSeek full traces---where 91.7\\% of samples are truncated---causes complete model collapse (28.5\\% accuracy, eval loss diverges to NaN), confirming that information density in the trace content, not the loss function, is the binding constraint on learning.\n\n\\paragraph{Exception events capture test verdict signals, not just crashes.}\nAlthough 55.5\\% of failures are silent logic errors, \\texttt{exception\\_only} achieves 100\\% fail recall because test assertions generate \\texttt{AssertionError} events, confirming ``exception events'' encompasses both crashes and assertion failures.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{training_dynamics}\n\\caption{Training dynamics comparison. Exception-only (blue) converges to 99.33\\% within 50 steps and saturates at 99.83\\% by step 150. Full-trace with label-only loss (red, dashed) oscillates between 82\\% and 89\\% across all 444 training steps without stable convergence, consistent with overfitting to spurious patterns in verbose trace content.}\n\\label{fig:training_dynamics}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Information density and learning dynamics.}\nFigure~\\ref{fig:training_dynamics} shows the contrast: \\texttt{exception\\_only} converges rapidly and saturates, while \\texttt{full\\_trace} with label-only loss oscillates throughout training---train loss approaches zero while eval accuracy stalls, a signature of overfitting to spurious patterns (Appendix~\\ref{sec:training_dynamics}).\nSeed variance negatively correlates with input density (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}), confirming compact inputs stabilize learning.\n",
        "numLines": 49,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 49
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "discussion.tex"
  },
  "type": "attachment",

        "content": "\\section{Conclusion}\n\nWe presented the first systematic component ablation for verdict prediction across four models (1.5B--13B, three families), finding that information density determines learning efficiency: compact exception events converge rapidly while verbose traces oscillate without stable convergence.\nException events achieve ${\\geq}$99.33\\% across all four models with consistent cross-model generalization, while lower-density loop signals are model-dependent; generalization beyond LLM-generated Python code remains open.\n",
